## Twenty-first stamp · the disposition proposal answered, all three items

Your proposal is absorbed and both of your stops were right. Stopping before item 2 rather than half-changing the locate charter is the correct trade and its recorded design means the next session builds instead of re-derives. Asking for the boundary before building creation semantics against it is the discipline working: **you built toward one answer and got the other, and nothing you built is wasted.**

**1 · RULED BY MATTHEW · THE MACHINE CREATES, WITH THE GATE IN FRONT.** Your §4 boundary is overruled. The Contact toggle keeps its ON default. The consequence you asked to have ruled explicitly, answered explicitly: **yes, Fatema appears on the map. Presence is the acceptance criterion, not merely the end of silence.** Your boundary proposal and its reasoning stay recorded in full and are not deleted; design's own position matched yours and was overruled with the record intact, which is the second time this ledger carries a design recommendation Matthew ruled against on the Contact question. The toggle survives with a narrower job: whether an approved person lands as a Contact or stays a shadow.

**2 · BUT CREATION IS THE TAIL CASE. RESOLUTION IS THE MAIN CASE, AND IT IS A DIVERGENCE FROM WRITTEN LAW.** Fatema already has a Contact and a map row: Matthew set her Political Status to Inner Circle at the 7 August map read, and `ALTF__Contact__c` is `nillable=false`, so a map row proves a Contact. **Her seven pairs did not drop for want of creation.** They dropped because call 2's closed candidate list is built from the roster and she is not on it. Charters §P8.1 already requires the candidate set to carry "every participant and every load-bearing mentioned person, matched against the closed candidate set (map rows, Contacts, shadows, roster), NONE or AMBIGUOUS where honest, never invented." **So the silent drop is the implementation diverging from charter law already written, not a law that was missing.** Build the candidate-set widening first, and **report a number before any creation code exists: of the 7 a23 pairs, how many resolve to an existing Contact, shadow, or map row with zero creation.** Design's expectation, recorded so it can be shown wrong: most of them, Fatema included. *(Twenty-second stamp: shown wrong, by design itself, before you spent the run. One of seven. See that stamp's item 3.)*

**3 · THE GATE IN FRONT IS NOT THE GATE YOU BUILT. This is a precondition, not a suggestion.** Your own finding names the hole and it is larger than you scoped it: the subject gate decides on email domain, and **a mentioned person never has an email, ever.** The gate is structurally blind on exactly the path Matthew has now opened. A transcript saying "Renee is handling that" mints a person-shaped subject for our own account director with nothing to catch it. That is the Wendy failure reproduced through the one door the gate cannot watch. **The mentioned-person path does not build until a name-based internal check exists**, run against the internal roster and the same admin-ratified role map Test 1 reads, with AMBIGUOUS honest. Recorded plainly so the argument is not miscarried forward: **Wendy is no longer the argument for anything, because your subject gate makes her structurally impossible. The argument is the next Wendy, who will be mentioned rather than present.** *(Twenty-second stamp: she has a name now. Pat, buq2, a real dropped pair.)*

**4 · CREATION ONLY ON LADDER-ZERO, AND NEVER ON AMBIGUOUS.** The identity ladder runs first, and creation is reachable only where it returns zero matches. AMBIGUOUS routes to the Identification flag, which exists as the fifth flag type and is built for exactly this; a link under ambiguity is never guessed and never created. Never-invented stands unchanged. The person-row boundary still gates entry: a load-bearing mention earns a row, a name dropped in chatter earns nothing, and the cardinality guards apply to this creation path as to every other.

**5 · NEW HAZARD, created by this ruling and named at the moment it is created · A MENTIONED PERSON'S NAME IS ASR OUTPUT.** A roster string is some system's record of a human. A mentioned name is what a speaker said and what a transcriber heard. "Fatema Choudray" and "Fatima Chowdhury" are one human and two Contacts, and **a Contact minted from a transcript spelling carries that transcription error into the customer's CRM permanently.** The byte check does not help here: a misheard name is not an invented quote, and the check that catches invented words cannot catch wrong ones. Build consequence: **match aggressively, create conservatively.** The deterministic name parse already ruled for roster strings applies, and the LastName requirement is a free structural guard, since a Contact without one cannot exist.

**6 · THE CREATE-LEG RECORD IS A PRECONDITION NOW, not the improvement you offered it as.** Your §2 recommendation is RATIFIED as proposed and for your reasons: the Contact stays, marked and reported as machine-created-and-disowned with its reason, and Matthew or the customer's admin decides deletion holding facts we do not have. The never-delete law protects our ledger because our ledger is evidence, and a row in the customer's CRM is not; but deleting a row someone may have since touched is the destructive move this build has refused three times, so mark-and-report is the honest disposition rather than a compromise. **What the auto-create ruling changes is volume.** A path that mints Contacts routinely makes "which rows did we put in this org, and which do we now disown" a question archaeology cannot answer. So the record you proposed building **ships before the creation leg does**, and it is the watermark law reaching creation: **a machine-created row we cannot enumerate is a row we can never disown.** *(Twenty-seventh stamp: it ships WITH the resolution-stage rebuild, and Dan Lewis is back-filled as its first row. Twenty-eighth stamp: it is also what makes the purge protocol lawful.)*

**7 · THE RETRACTION DEFECT · fix it as ordinary work, under one binding constraint.** Design's default under the delegated mode, Matthew's veto open, and the reasoning recorded because you were right that it deserves one: the standing law already says a non-blank value our watermark does not claim was put there by a human, which entails that a value our watermark **does** claim is ours. The code diverged from the law; fixing it restores the law rather than changing it, so it is ordinary work and does not need a ruling. **THE CONSTRAINT IS THE WHOLE OF IT: `touched` must mean OUR OWN RECORDED PROJECTION WATERMARK for that dimension, never the native `ALTF__*_Last_Modified__c` stamp.** The native stamps are non-null for precisely the rows a human edited. **Fatema's Political Status carries one, hers.** If `touched` reads the native stamp, her row reads as touched and this repair silently undoes the session-77 repair, which is the correction-that-disables-what-it-corrects hazard firing inside the correction for its own first instance. **Ship it with a standing regression assertion: a row carrying a native stamp and no watermark of ours still holds back, Fatema's row the specimen.** Then Wendy's Coverage retracts and her correction completes. *(Thirty-seventh stamp: shown wrong by evidence; the value carries no watermark of ours and lawfully holds back; disposition is Matthew's by hand.)* *(Twenty-sixth stamp: shipped and ratified; the retraction repair is closed.)*

**8 · Recorded with credit, because the record is the point.** The stamp repair's reasoning correction is the sharper version and it generalises: the scope key and output hash were never buggy not because they are computed but because no caller assigns them, so they are absent from the sObject's field map and an update cannot carry them. Refusing a caller-supplied stamp loudly rather than overwriting it is the only form of the rule that cannot recreate the stale copy it exists to prevent. And the sequencing reason you gave for 3 before 1 before 4 is correct and worth keeping: a red suite makes every later claim of passing tests worthless, and the subject gate must precede the shadow path because mentioned-person resolution mints person-shaped subjects for anyone the artifact names, sellers included.

**Order from here, yours within it:** the retraction repair under the constraint, which unblocks Wendy and is the only thing standing on a live wrong value; then the candidate-set widening with its zero-creation number; then item 2, the criterion name, whose design is already recorded; then the name-based internal check; then creation, behind the create-leg record. *(Revised by the twenty-second stamp, which inserts the refusal diagnosis second and moves the criterion name up. Revised again by the twenty-third, which answers the diagnosis. Revised again by the twenty-fifth, which pulls the live-org test to the front. The twenty-sixth stamp was the prior order; the twenty-seventh and twenty-eighth are current.)*

3. **The fresh full run end to end**: call 0, sweep with the guard, join, projection; regression report before projection; row export and timings with the report; run id to Matthew.

**[Thirty-third stamp, execution session, parked] TWO-READ CONFIDENCE IS GRADED, NOT BINARY, and the open question is whether the marker is load-bearing.** Matthew, 9 August: a pair only one of the two reads returned is NOT discarded; it stands, at LOWER CONFIDENCE than a pair both reads returned, the more so because the two reads never quote byte-for-byte, so agreement is meaning plus byte-range intersection, never string equality. This confirms the twenty-seventh stamp ("corroboration is a marker, never a gate") and machine sheet 3.5, and opens the design question under them: the corroborated-versus-single marker is recorded, but is it USED anywhere (call 3 ordering or bar, projection, flags, a displayed confidence)? Options with costs for a strong session: (a) leave it recorded and unused, zero cost, but then "lower confidence" is a label with no effect; (b) let confidence order or gate call 3 or flagging, a judgment-layer change that waits for the accuracy phase; (c) surface confidence on the map or receipt, a projection-surface change. Not built, not chosen; efficacy topic, deferred behind the efficiency work.

**[Thirty-third stamp, cont'd · Matthew's answer, 9 August]** On whether the confidence marker is used: not yet, but it likely lands at CALL 3, by MERGING claims that share the same question and answer keys so the verifier reconciles and upholds more readily, rather than sending the same-key question-and-answer pairs to call 3 duplicated and blind. His lean is option (b), scoped to call-3 claim-merging. A strong session rules the mechanism and reconciles it with the twenty-seventh stamp's merge key, so a call-3 merge on shared keys never collapses two claims that share bytes and question but carry different meanings, which still ride to call 3 independently.

**[Thirty-fourth stamp, execution session, parked] THE KEYED-VERIFY GRAMMAR-SIZE CEILING needs a split trigger of its own.** pf0808-i1 measured it: verify's keyed strict-tool schema hit a model-gateway 400, "compiled grammar too large, simplify your tool schemas or reduce the number of strict tools," when the plain bucket carried about 24 claims (shardCount=1); h1's 22-claim bucket stayed under. shardCount=2 (two 12-claim shards) cleared it. So shardCount is a required function of claims-per-bucket, not a tuning knob, and there is a second split trigger beside the twenty-ninth stamp's 90-second wall trigger: a grammar-size trigger keyed on claim count per bucket. Options with costs for a strong session: (a) auto-drive shardCount from a claims-per-bucket threshold measured to sit safely under the grammar limit, cheap and mechanical, needs the threshold pinned by measurement; (b) shrink the keyed verify schema itself (fewer strict fields per call), which reduces pressure but touches the verify contract; (c) both. Driven by hand today, not built as a trigger, not chosen. Binds before any run denser than Project Farma. *(Thirty-fifth stamp: built ahead of ruling by CODE at 8936be9; adjudicated and CLOSED there.)*

**[Thirty-fourth stamp, parked] CALL 0's COLD-FLAKE needs a named retry policy.** Third instance across tg1, the halving era, and i1: call 0 answers yes on a cold call with no quote, the charter's quote-law refuses it, a single retry resolves it. Options: (a) a bounded single retry on the quote-law refusal, which the express lane's speculative launch can absorb at no wall cost; (b) leave it manual. Not ruled. The join DML wall (about 4.2 rows per claim, the 150 sync ceiling near 35 claims) is the sixteenth stamp's known wall now measured; its mitigations (caller-side join split, single projection) are already ruled and queued, so it is noted, not newly parked. *(Thirty-fifth stamp: the retry was built ahead of ruling at 8936be9; adjudicated and CLOSED there.)*

*(Forty-first stamp: RESOLVED. Matthew ruled option (a) in conversation, 11 August: call 3 moves to the configured Haiku; temperature is feature-detected and optional permanently; N-of-M voting is dead on cost. See the forty-first stamp.)* **[Fortieth stamp, execution session, parked] MODEL SEPARATION ON THE §P8 PATH: the verifier is the reader's own model, and the temperature remedy is unavailable without changing it.** `pf0811-t0` measured it from the wire: call 3's temperature-0 probe cannot run because `claude-opus-5` (and `claude-sonnet-5`) refuse the `temperature` parameter at the gateway with HTTP 400, "deprecated for this model," while only `claude-haiku-4-5` accepts it. Call 3 dispatches on `claude-opus-5` because every §P8 stage builds from `inventorySpec()`; the configured `AAO_Verify_Model__c` (haiku-4-5) is read only by `verifySpec()` on the older `AAO_EBV` path, and `AAO_Extract.requireSeparateModels()` is called from `AAO_EBV` only, never from §P8 (grep: two callers, both `AAO_EBV`, plus its test). Call 3's BLINDNESS holds structurally (no person field, no transcript, per `AAO_VerifyPairsCharter`); MODEL SEPARATION does not, on the path where verification actually happens. Options with costs for a strong session, CODE's, none chosen and none built: (a) point call 3 at the configured haiku-4-5, which restores separation AND makes the temperature probe runnable, but changes the verifier on every measurement this project holds, so the graded numbers (tg1 87.5 percent, Pass 1 80.0 percent) do not carry forward, and the thirty-seventh stamp's small-model caveat applies, "one adjudicated comparison against the strong model is owed before its rejections are believed"; note this is the SAME change as the long-planned call-3 downsize comparison (thirtieth stamp item 1c, "the largest remaining lever on both seconds and dollars"), so (a) spends the a23 downsize adjudication already owed rather than a new one; (b) extend `requireSeparateModels()` to the §P8 path, cheap to write but it throws on every run until (a) or an explicit exemption is ruled, so it is a ruling and not a fix; (c) rule the current same-model shape correct in writing (blindness carrying independence) and correct the config so it stops naming models the pass does not use, cheapest and it makes the ledger honest, but it accepts that "second reader" means second reading rather than second model. The temperature remedy is entangled with this: there is no temperature-0 probe of call 3 that does not also change the verifier model. N-of-M voting, the thirty-eighth stamp's named reserve, is model-independent and carries no such entanglement. This closes nothing; People's last gate stays open until this is ruled and the chosen remedy measured. Not built, not chosen.

**[Sixty-sixth stamp, execution session, parked] THE RUN RECEIPT OBJECT HAS A SECOND CONSUMER, and the Inspector's performance toggle currently reports a gap instead of a number.** The twenty-ninth stamp CONFIRMED the receipt ("ONE thin object, one row per Source per pass") carrying per-stage wall milliseconds, calls made, split events, COLD or WARM, and charter and normalizer versions, and left the build timing to CODE ("Not needed tonight... Build it with or immediately after the rebuild, your call"). It was never built. The sixty-sixth stamp's item 5 asks for per-stage wall time and governor consumption behind a toggle "showing only numbers the run actually journalled, nothing recomputed on the page", and NOTHING IN THE ORG JOURNALS THEM: those numbers live only in each run's written report under `review/`. The toggle is therefore built and renders, and it says exactly what is missing and why, because deriving a stage timing from record timestamps would be the recomputation the stamp forbids. Options with costs for a strong session, none chosen and none built: **(a)** build the receipt as the twenty-ninth stamp specced it, cost one object against the enterprise install budget that same stamp made law plus a write on the pass's hot path, benefit the toggle fills, the did-we-read-it record finally exists, and a run report stops being the only place performance lives; **(b)** journal per-stage numbers onto the existing `AAO_Source__c` row, zero new objects but the grain is wrong (a Source is read by many passes) and it needs a JSON blob nobody can query; **(c)** leave it unbuilt and let the toggle keep reporting the gap, zero cost, and the Inspector cannot stand in for the run report during testing, which was the stated reason for the toggle. Design's own note at the twenty-ninth stamp still applies unchanged: every new object justifies itself against the enterprise install budget.

**[Seventieth stamp, execution session, parked · URGENT, THE DEAL IS BARE] CALL 2 CANNOT COMPLETE ITS ENVELOPE ON TODAY'S MODEL, and Project Farma is purged with the rerun blocked four stages in.** Matthew authorized a purge and full rerun. The purge ran and is verified (cards card-first so the 70 child relationships cascaded rather than being deleted by hand; both sections survive including his "Enter title" column; 292 pairs, 23 claims, 23 candidates, 13 answers, 3 map rows removed across the three historic run keys; human-watermark guard passed; three retired criteria kept and reported; seeds, Emerson and the other 126 map rows untouched). Calls 0 and 1 then ran clean: call 0 cleared first try with no cold-flake, 11.6 s; read 1 located 32 COLD; read 2 located 35 WARM at 27,534 cache-read tokens, the cache prefix reorder paying exactly as approved. **THE FIRST GENUINE RUN RECEIPT EXISTS**, written by the pass rather than seeded: 3 stages, 92,659 ms serial, 3 callouts, worst 40,676 ms, WARM, normalizer NF1+raw:75917ba2. **CALL 2 THEN BLOCKED THE RUN.** Handed 67 pairs it disposed 1 and `assertOneForOne` threw, which is the guard working. Diagnosed to the wire and NOT a guess: not batch size (15 and 5 both return 1); NOT TRUNCATION (`stop=end_turn`, out=324, thinking=100); it is the generation DEGENERATING INTO DUPLICATES — handed 3 refs it returned 4 entries with one ref repeated and a blank basis, and the pair key's unique index then collapses them, so two independent mechanisms each caught half. The schema was checked and is correct (the `pair_ref` enum carries exactly the handed refs). **AND THE CAPABILITY CLAIM IN OUR OWN COMMENT WAS RE-VERIFIED RATHER THAN TRUSTED**: structured outputs still reject an array `minItems` other than 0 or 1, re-measured today, request id `req_011Cdzv8W2273F78HtUSV7i8`, so the structural enforcement is genuinely unavailable exactly as recorded 5 August. **Nothing this session touched call 2**; its charter, schema and prompt are byte-identical to the last successful pass. THE STRUCTURAL OBSERVATION, offered as evidence and not as a decision: call 3 and the card-face call do not have this problem because they use a KEYED object, one property per unit with every key in `required`, so the envelope is enforced structurally; call 2 is the one keyed-grain call still using an array with a prose description. Options with costs, none chosen and none built: **(a)** convert call 2 to the keyed shape, which enforces §P8.2's already-written envelope law and rides infrastructure already ratified (`MAX_UNITS_PER_KEYED_CALL`, `keyedShardCount`, and the thirty-fifth stamp's extension of chunking to the resolution leg on the reasoning that the grammar ceiling is a property of every keyed strict schema), cost being a mid-run change to a model call's contract, the class of move already adjudicated a breach once, plus roughly 5 calls on this stage instead of 1; **(b)** batch of 1, structurally certain and 67 callouts, which is the fifty-nine-callout disease the twenty-seventh stamp deleted, rejected on that ruling and named so nobody re-proposes it; **(c)** retry on a short return, which is a call retrying by growing and the charter forbids it; **(d)** dispatch call 2 on a different configured model, which BYO-LLM makes configuration rather than code, costing an adjudicated comparison before its dispositions are believed. **THE LOCATED PAIRS ARE BANKED (67), so whichever is ruled the resumed run starts at stage 4 and does not re-pay calls 0 or 1.** The deal has no map rows and no cards until this is ruled and the pass finishes; it cannot be restored by hand because the harvest it would restore from is what the purge removed. Report and rows in `review/pf0813-uphold-rerun/`.

**[Seventy-ninth stamp, execution session, parked · A RULING IS OWED] THE MERGE RULING AND THE REINFORCEMENT COUNTER ARE INDIVIDUALLY RIGHT AND INTERACT WRONGLY, measured on the Emerson stacked run.** Reinforcement fired for the first time since the fiftieth stamp: 20 Established, 17 Reinforced. **7 OF THOSE 17 QUOTE BYTE RANGES ALREADY ON THE ANSWER** — identical source, identical offsets, identical words (Neeraja Chimata's `AAO_PB_GOAL` carries two claims both quoting `23993-24064`). The sixty-second stamp already ruled the distinction for cards: "A RERUN COUNTS UNCHANGED, NEVER REINFORCED, because a rerun of the same words is not the buyer saying it twice - that distinction protects the reinforcement count's meaning." Two READS of one transcript finding one utterance is that same thing, and reinforcement is a count guidance ORDERS BY, so an inflated one is a lie about how often somebody pressed something. **WHY THE MERGE DID NOT STOP IT, and why this is a ruling rather than a fix:** the twenty-seventh stamp's merge key is contract plus byte intersection plus SAME VOICED MEANING, and it says explicitly that "same bytes and question with different meanings: both ride to call 3 blind, independently" - so two pairs surviving on identical bytes is DELIBERATE and correct at verify. What nobody priced is the join, where the answer key is subject plus contract, so those two deliberately-separate meanings collapse onto ONE answer and the second claim reads as a repetition. Options with costs, none chosen and nothing built: **(a)** reinforcement requires NEW BYTES, a claim whose spans are already wholly on the answer counts Unchanged exactly as the card writer already does, cheap and deterministic and it makes the counter mean its name, cost being that two genuinely different meanings on one utterance stop being countable as two which may be what the mixed-evidence law wants to keep; **(b)** reinforcement requires A DIFFERENT OCCASION, only a claim from another Source reinforces, the strongest reading of "the buyer said it again", cost being that a person who presses the same point three times in one call counts once; **(c)** leave it and document, zero cost and the number stays inflated by a factor set by the READ COUNT rather than by the buyer. Report and rows in `review/em0813-stack/`.

**[Seventy-ninth stamp, execution session, recorded] CROSS-CALL COMPLETENESS ACCUMULATION IS STILL UNEXERCISED ON REAL SPEECH.** The Emerson stacked run produced ZERO answers carrying spans from more than one call and zero carrying claims from more than one call. The seventieth stamp's union works and is proven by test on the seed fixture; these two real calls simply never produced a partial establishment that a later call completed. Recorded so nobody reads the stacked run as having exercised it, and noted as exactly what the Wells Fargo authored corpus is directed to walk deliberately.

**[Eightieth stamp, execution session, parked · A RULING IS OWED] THE TWENTY-SEVENTH STAMP'S MERGE IS BUILT AND IS NOT ON THE DRIVEN PATH, and it is the other half of the same-bytes story.** `AAO_Resolve` — the deterministic resolution stage carrying the two-read merge, the designator ladder and the corroboration marker — **has ZERO callers in production code**; grep across every non-test class returns nothing. The pass drives `AAO_Pass.identify`, which is the model call over every located pair, and never the deterministic legs. Measured and unambiguous: **0 of 67 identified pairs across `em0813-stack-c1` and `em0813-stack-c2` carry `AAO_Corroborated__c = true`.** Not a low rate, zero. The specimen is exact: `r1q10` and `r2q7` on the 29 July call both quote **8322-8371**, both read meaning `SIGNATURE_APPROVER`, both name designator "Fatima" — same contract, identical bytes, identical meaning — and `AAO_Resolve.mergePlan` would have collapsed them into one canonical row marked Corroborated. This does NOT weaken the eightieth stamp's ruling, which is right that nobody priced the join and whose join-side rule is the correct backstop either way, because same-bytes-DIFFERENT-meaning pairs are MEANT to survive the merge and still land on one answer. What it adds is that the stage which would have caught it one step earlier is not wired in. Options with costs, none chosen and nothing built: **(a)** wire `AAO_Resolve` in front of `AAO_Pass.identify` as the twenty-seventh stamp designed it, so the deterministic legs dispose what they can and the model leg takes only the remainder — this is the ruled shape and it also restores the corroboration marker and the given-name rung to the live path, cost being a change to the pass's stage order and a re-measure of call 2's batch arithmetic; **(b)** leave the drive as it is and rely on the join-side rule alone, zero cost today, but corroboration stays a field nothing writes and the twenty-seventh stamp's merge stays dead code that reads as live; **(c)** retire `AAO_Resolve` explicitly with its reason, cheapest to read and it throws away a built deterministic stage that the notes and emails channel will need, since a note carries no roster and the designator path is the ONLY path there. Report and rows in `review/em0814-reinforcement/`.

**[Eightieth stamp, execution session, parked] DISOWNING AND PURGE-ENUMERABILITY POINT OPPOSITE WAYS, and the code follows the twenty-first stamp rather than the eightieth.** The eightieth stamp says the created Contact is "MARKED AND DISOWNED... and enumerable for the sandbox purge exactly because the record carries it." `AAO_Purge` deletes machine-created Contacts `WHERE AAO_Disowned__c = false`, so **marking the twin disowned makes the purge SKIP it.** The filter is not a bug: it is the twenty-first stamp's ruling verbatim, "the Contact stays, marked and reported as machine-created-and-disowned with its reason, and Matthew or the customer's admin decides deletion holding facts we do not have." Disowned means we stop touching it, deletion included. Nothing was changed on a hunch. Options: **(a)** the twenty-first stamp governs and the eightieth's phrase is loose, so a disowned row leaves only by a human's hand, zero build, cost being that the Fatima twin stands in the sandbox until somebody removes it; **(b)** the sandbox purge is exempted from the disown filter, small and it makes the eightieth stamp's sentence true, cost being that the one flag now means two different things depending on which org it runs in; **(c)** a second flag separating "we disown this" from "this may be swept in a sandbox", honest and it is one more field on the create-leg record. **The twin's reach, measured before any disposition because no delete on a vendor object happens without it: 1 map row, 2 card-contact links, 1 participant, 1 answer, and Contact carries 184 child relationships in this org. Nothing was deleted.**

**[Eightieth stamp, execution session, recorded, not a defect] THE GIVEN-NAME RUNG DOES NOT LINK FATIMA TO FATEMA, and while the twin stands it links to the twin.** Tried from the runtime rather than reasoned about. The rung matches EXACTLY, as every rung on that ladder always has, and the CRM carries `Fatema` against the transcript's `Fatima`, one character apart. So the stamp's "re-attaches if and only if the rebuilt ladder links it" resolves to: it does not re-attach. Fuzzy matching is not ruled and was not invented. **What closed this defect is the creation bar, not the rung**, and with the twin still on the account the rung resolves "Fatima" TO THE TWIN (`resolved by single token as given or family name, within the account` → `003WD00001RCfLzYAL`). An aggressive matcher finds a minted duplicate as readily as a real person: the rung cannot un-make a duplicate, it can only stop the next one. Recorded so nobody reads Fatema as an acceptance case for the rung; she is the acceptance case for the BAR, and the rung's own acceptance case is a lone token matching a real given name, which is tested.
